revenues and sales proceeds generated by the ETS facility will be split 75/25 between customers 
and Company shareholders.  Non-labor O&M costs associated with operating the ETS, which 
will increase or decrease depending on customer use of the facility once built, will be deferred as 
well as the customers’ portion of the revenue from the facility.  Any net balance will be paid to 
or recovered from customers through a surcharge mechanism.

concessions among multiple parties.  As explained above the Company initially proposed

expressed concern.  In recognition of those concerns, the Joint Proposal appropriately requires 
Company shareholders to fund the gas-related efforts associated with the ETS facility, and bars 
the Company from engaging in using the ETS facility to blend hydrogen into the gas distribution 
system until and unless such efforts are demonstrated to be safe to the Commission’s

17.5 
Online Fuel Switching Calculator 
 
 
In its direct testimony, the Company proposed to implement a new web-based 
fuel-switching calculator tool that would help inform customers about the energy savings and 
financial implications associated with switching from the customer’s existing fuel source to an 
alternative fuel source, including geothermal heat pumps and air-source heat pumps.593  The 
Company proposed that the cost of the Fuel Switching Calculator be shared with its downstate 
affiliates, KEDNY and KEDLI.594  In addition to O&M expenses associated with operating the 
Fuel Switching Calculator,595 the Company proposed to hire one FTE to support the program.596  
In its pre-filed testimony, Staff supported the Company’s proposed Fuel Switching Calculator,597 
but did not agree that an incremental FTE was necessary since KEDNY and KEDLI sought 
appropriate incremental labor to implement the program already.598 
 
 
The Joint Proposal allows the Company to implement its proposed Fuel Switching 
Calculator and reflects a level of revenue requirement associated with this project that spreads 
the costs of such program to all National Grid affiliates providing comparable gas distribution 
services.  In addition, the Joint Proposal reflects the level of FTEs proposed by Staff.  The Joint 
Proposal is reasonable and in the public interest, as the Fuel Switching Calculator will be a 
valuable outreach and education tool for customers considering switching fuel sources to air-
source or geothermal heat pumps.

17.6 
Economic Development Programs 
17.6.1 Economic Development Fund 
 
 
The Company proposed to continue offering its economic development discount 
programs with modifications to the Excelsior Jobs Program (EJP), which would require all 
applicants to explore energy efficiency options through the Company, NYSERDA, and/or other 
entities.599  The Company also proposed to continue the annual reconciliation of expenditures for 
both the Empire Zone Rate (EZP) and the EJP.600  In its testimony, Staff agreed with these 
proposals, as they are consistent with statewide energy-efficiency initiatives.601 
 
 
The Joint Proposal contains these proposals, which should be adopted by the 
Commission because they integrate environmental planning into the Company’s economic 
development programs while ensuring that the programs continue to be funded.   
17.6.2 Economic Development Grant Program 
 
 
The Company proposed in testimony to continue offering its suite of economic 
development grant programs with no change to its annual budget of $11 million for electric and 
$1 million for gas,602 subject to a downward-only reconciliation over the term of the rate plan.603  
In its testimony, Staff agreed with a downward-only reconciliation for a multi-year rate plan.604 
 
 
The Joint Proposal stipulates the adoption of these recommendations.  These 
terms are reasonable because these programs promote economic development, which attracts and 
retains businesses to the service territory that create and retain jobs.  These programs are a 
benefit to qualifying businesses and to the communities within the Company’s service territory 
and therefore should be adopted.

The Joint Proposal stipulates that the Company will provide a report to the 
Department no later than April 1, of each year which details the economic development activities

17.8 
Unspent Energy Efficiency Funds 
 
 
In its direct testimony, Staff noted that there is a significant balance of unspent 
EEPS, ETIP, and NYSERDA Clean Energy Fund and Bill-As-You-Go collections (i.e., energy 
efficiency program funds that were collected prior to the beginning of the NE:NY in 2019, or 
“pre-NE:NY funds”),617 and stated that, while Staff was not making any specific 
recommendations related to use of such funds, such funds could be used to offset the revenue 
requirement increases in these proceedings.618  NRDC and Sierra Club recommended that 
instead of using the pre-NE:NY funds to offset recovery of the already-authorized energy 
efficiency budgets, such funds should be used to increase the Company’s energy efficiency and 
electrification offerings above and beyond the levels already authorized.619 
 
 
The Joint Proposal recognizes that there remain deferred unspent pre-NE:NY 
funds, and requires the Company to use such funds to offset incremental NE:NY activity 
authorized by the Commission as a result of the Interim Review process discussed above, as well 
as incremental energy efficiency budgets authorized by the Commission, but not already 
reflected in base rates during the Stayout Period.  As discussed above, the Joint Proposal is 
reasonable since it conforms with Commission directives and provides flexibility for the 
Company to be able to respond to outcomes of the Interim Review process offsetting the need for 
new collections from customers.  The Joint Proposal is reasonable, is in the public interest, and 
should therefore be approved.

18. 
Gas Matters 
18.1 
Reducing Billed Gas Usage / Non-Pipe Alternatives 
 
 
The Joint Proposal includes provisions setting out a goal for the Company to 
reduce weather-normalized gas usage in its service territory throughout the term of the rate plan 
below forecast levels, with specific goals to achieve a net-zero increase in billed gas usage 
during RY2 and RY3 as compared to RY1.  As potential means to achieve this level of gas usage 
reduction, the Joint Proposal requires the Company to implement a number of new efforts 
beyond the Commission-approved energy efficiency and demand response programs.  First, the 
Joint Proposal requires that the Company terminate its programs and marketing efforts 
promoting new gas connections or conversions, not including material related to promoting the 
Company’s authorized gas energy efficiency programs.  Second, the Joint Proposal requires the 
Company to develop educational and marketing material to promote alternative non-gas heating 
options, such as geothermal and air-source heat pumps.  Third, the Joint Proposal commits the 
Company to spend up to $1 million of shareholder funds to provide incentives to encourage gas-
to-heat pump conversions. 
 
 
Fourth, the Joint Proposal requires Company to attempt to implement NPA 
projects in lieu of LPP replacement projects and new gas customer connections, as well as 
integrate the NPA screening and associated processes into the Company’s capital planning 
processes.  Specifically, the Joint Proposal requires the Company to identify areas of its gas 
distribution system slated for LPP replacement where non-gas NPAs could be used to address 
customers’ heating needs and gas services requests that involve a main extension of more than 
500 feet and would serve five or more customers.  In such areas, the Company must survey 
affected customers to determine interest in such an NPA, and issue RFPs to procure to support 
the potential NPA projects if material customer interest exists and such customers are willing to 
consider alternatives to natural gas.  Beginning in RY2, the Company would be required to 
annual identify at least five segments in LPP which could be removed in all customers’ natural 
gas loads are met with cost-effective NPAs, allowing such sections of LPP to be removed or 
abandoned, counting any lengths of removed or abandoned LPP toward the Company’s LPP 
replacement metrics.  The Joint Proposal requires the Company to file project-specific benefit 
cost analyses and incentive mechanism proposals until such time as standard procedures are 
developed as part of the ongoing Gas Planning Procedures Proceeding or another statewide 


--- Page 153 ---
CASES 20-E-0380 et al. 
 
 
146 
generic proceeding.  Under the terms of the Joint Proposal, the Company is also allowed to 
petition the Commission for approval of a program to provide potential customers requesting 
new gas service with incentives against the cost of such new gas connections which would be 
used for heat pump installations. 
 
 
In addition to the above-described measures, the Joint Proposal includes a number 
of reports and studies.  First, the Joint Proposal requires the Company to complete a study 
evaluating how the Company’s gas business, and its affiliates KEDNY and KEDLI, may evolve 
in the future to support the emission reduction and renewable energy goals of the CLCPA and 
any emission reduction goals the Company has independently adopted by March 31, 2023 
(CLCPA Study).  Parties will have the opportunity to provide feedback on the CLCPA study at 
three intervals: prior to the study being formally started to discuss the scope of the study, once a 
draft of the study results is available but before the study is formally filed, and a final meeting 
just prior to the study’s publication.  Under the terms of the Joint Proposal, the Company is 
required to include the investments, programs, and initiatives which have been added, removed, 
or altered to achieve the objectives included in the CLCPA Study as part of its next rate filing.  
Under the Joint Proposal, the Company will be allowed to defer up to $500,000 of third-party 
costs in aggregate split between Niagara Mohawk, KEDNY, and KEDLI. 
 
 
Second, the Joint Proposal requires Company to complete and file a study on the 
potential depreciation impacts of climate change policies and laws on the Company’s gas asset’s 
at least three months before its next base rate filing, with the same three opportunities for 
stakeholder feedback as provided in the CLCPA study.  Third, as part of the Company’s next rate 
filing the Joint Proposal requires the Company to calculate a baseline greenhouse gas emissions 
profile, determine the current annual greenhouse gas emissions associated with its gas network, 
assess the greenhouse gas impacts of the investments, programs, and initiatives included in such 
rate filing, and provide an analysis of the NPAs considered for each investment, program, or 
initiative with an explanation if an NPA option was not selected. 
 
 
All of the above-described provisions of the Joint Proposal advances gas policy 
matters related to reducing GHG emissions associated with gas usage and the Company’s gas 
network, For context regarding the heat pump related provisions, in litigation, the Company 
proposed that they undertake an Network Geothermal Program in which the Company would 
own and install the ground-loop portion of geothermal systems in parts of their service 


--- Page 154 ---
CASES 20-E-0380 et al. 
 
 
147 
territories.620  During litigation, Pace and AGREE621 and NYGEO622 supported the Company’s 
proposal, whereas Staff opposed it due to concerns with the gas utility owning and operating 
non-gas plant.623  While the Joint Proposal does not recommend that the Company move forward 
with a further Network Geothermal Program, it does provide a number of measures to support 
heat pump adoption in the Company’s service territory.  The Joint Proposal’s recommendations 
regarding these issues appropriately balances the litigation positions of various parties.  
Furthermore, the recommendations take significant steps toward meeting the State’s climate and 
energy policy goals.  Accordingly, these provisions are reasonable and should be adopted. 
18.2 
Gas Supply Matters – Non-Core Daily Balanced Customers 
 
 
In initial testimony, the Company proposed to close its non-core daily balancing 
program to new customers and offer a new core daily balancing transportation service.624  The 
proposed core daily balancing transportation service would allow any new non-core type 
customer to subscribe and be treated as a core customer so their agent or marketer can obtain the 
balancing services from the Company.  Staff recommended that the Company be required to 
implement a process to verify that direct non-core customers or the ESCO’s or marketers service 
non-core customers have primary point capacity to the city gates so that non-core customer 
requirements are properly being met.625  In its rebuttal testimony, the Company argued that it 
cannot identify any non-core supply shortfalls and therefore cannot terminate any non-core 
customer in such a pool, requiring the Company to utilize its own capacity to safeguard its 
system during period of peak demand.626  This Highlights that the Company is at risk for supply 
shortfalls by non-core customers and that the Company’s proposal seeks to mitigate the risk. 
 
 
The Joint Proposal provides that the Company will retain its existing non-core 
daily balancing service for existing customers.  New firm non-core daily balanced customers, or 
an ESCO acting as a customer’s supplier, will not be permitted to commence service absent 
 
620  Direct Testimony of Niagara Mohawk Future of Heat Panel, pp. 29 – 31. 
621  Direct Testimony of Pace and AGREE Witness Acton, p. 24. 
622  Letter filed by NYGEO, November 24, 2020, p. 1. 
623  Direct Testimony of Staff Efficiency and Sustainability Panel, p. 60. 
624 Direct Testimony of Niagara Mohawk Gas Supply Panel, pp. 31. 
625 Direct Testimony of Staff Gas Reliability Panel, p. 11. 
626 Rebuttal Testimony of Niagara Mohawk Gas Supply Panel, p. 4. 


--- Page 155 ---
CASES 20-E-0380 et al. 
 
 
148 
proof that it has contracted for sufficient firm primary point upstream capacity to the Company’s 
city gate delivery point or points to serve the customer’s anticipated peak day requirements for at 
least one year and will be renewed annually to remain a firm customer. 
 
 
Additionally, within 180 days of a Commission order adopting the terms of the 
Joint Proposal, the Company is required to complete an audit of direct customers taking firm 
transportation service and ESCOs providing service to customers taking firm transportation 
service to determine what, if any portion of the loads is not being served with upstream primary 
point pipeline capacity to the Company’s citygate.  Once it has completed the audit, the 
Company will convene a meeting of all interested parties to determine what actions may be 
necessary with respect to the reliability of upstream delivery to existing firm, non-core, 
customers.  Further, the Company must issue a report with findings and recommendations within 
one year of a Commission order adopting the terms of the Joint Proposal.  The Joint Proposal 
provides for the continuation of non-core daily balancing service for both existing and new 
customers going forward while ensuring that the service will not diminish system reliability to 
core customers.  Accordingly, this provision of the Joint Proposal is reasonable and should be 
adopted. 
19. 
Filing for New Rates 
19.1 
During the Term of the Rate Plans 
The Company commits to not file for new base delivery rates to be effective prior 
to July 1, 2024.  This provision includes a number of standard exceptions, which allow for 
consideration of modifications that result in de minimis impact on rates, to address new services, 
or should the Commission determine that unforeseen circumstances have had a substantial 
impact on the Company’s rates of return so as to render the return unreasonable, unnecessary or 
inadequate for the provision of safe and adequate service.  This provision is a standard provision 
of rate proceeding joint proposals, is reasonable and should be adopted. 
19.2 
Following the Term of the Rate Plan 
If the Company does not file for new rates to become effective on July 1, 2024, 
certain provisions will take effect for the duration of the Stayout Period and cease on March 31, 
2025 or the date new rates become effective, whichever is sooner.  First, the Company’s Net 
Utility Plant and Depreciation Expense Reconciliation Mechanism for both electric and gas will 


--- Page 156 ---
CASES 20-E-0380 et al. 
 
 
149 
be converted to a two-way reconciliation capped at the Joint Proposal’s specified forecast levels 
for the twelve months ending March 31, 2025.  In conjunction with this modification, the 
Company would be allowed to establish a surcharge to recover the pre-tax revenue requirement 
associated with the net utility plant and depreciation levels to the extent they exceed the levels 
reflected in rates.  Second, the Company would be allowed to recover through a surcharge any 
Commission-approved NE:NY budget or approved energy efficiency programs not otherwise 
recovered in rates to achieve the energy efficiency targets in the nine months ending March 31, 
2025. 
The Company cannot avail itself of these mechanisms to the extent the 
mechanisms would cause the Company to earn a ROE in excess of 9.0 percent during the period 
in which the mechanisms are in effect.  In addition, the Company will use any net deferred 
credits, that are not otherwise committed to a specific program or have not been designated for a 
specific purpose by the Commission, in excess of $50 million for its electric operations and $25 
million for its gas operations, to offset any potential surcharges.  Further, the Company will not 
surcharge any incremental energy efficiency costs to the extent it has unspent energy efficiency 
funds from prior NE:NY years or pre-NENY funds available to offset such increased costs. 
These provisions are reasonable as they could alleviate the need for the Company 
to request base rate increases for both its electric and gas operations to be effective prior to 
April 1, 2025.  In addition, the Joint Proposal sets forth sufficient guardrails to ensure these 
mechanisms are only triggered when necessary and that the amounts subject to recovery from 
customers are limited, appropriate, and reasonable.  Specifically, these mechanisms cannot 
become effective unless certain conditions have been met and if they do become effective certain 
measures have been established to protect customers.  Thus, these provisions are reasonable and 
should be adopted. 
20. 
Corporate Structure and Affiliate Rules 
 
 
This section refers to Appendix 16 to the Joint Proposal, which includes the 
Company’s corporate structure and affiliate rules.  Appendix 16 to the Joint Proposal, with a few 
non-substantive clarifications, maintains the existing affiliate rules that are currently in effect for 
the Company.  This section ensures that these provisions remain in force and are easily 
accessible.  Accordingly, this section is reasonable and should be adopted. 


--- Page 157 ---
CASES 20-E-0380 et al. 
 
 
150 
21. 
Other Provisions 
 
 
This section contains standard provisions common to all rate proceeding joint 
proposals.  The provisions include a request that the Commission adopt the Joint Proposal 
without modification, a statement that the parties may pursue their own interests if the 
Commission does not adopt the Joint Proposal’s terms, the Joint Proposal’s lack of precedential 
value, an agreement to cooperate on future actions necessary to effectuate the Joint Proposal, and 
a statement of which elements will continue beyond the term of the Joint Proposal’s rate plan.  
These terms are standard, non-controversial elements of the Joint Proposal that do not affect the 
interests of ratepayers. 
IV. 
CONCLUSION 
 
 
For the reasons stated in Staff’s Statement in Support of the Joint Proposal and in 
this Reply Statement, Staff recommends that the Commission adopt the terms of the Joint 
Proposal without modification. 
 
 
Respectfully submitted, 
 
 
 
 
Brandon F. Goodrich 
Joseph Dowling 
Staff Counsel
